```python
class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        tot_apples = 0
        self.pfsum_row = []
        self.pfsum_col = []
        
        for i in range(self.r):
            pfr = 0
            pfs_r = [0] * self.c
            pfs_c = [0] * self.c
            for j in range(self.c):
                if i > 0:
                    pfs_c[j] += self.pfsum_col[i - 1][j]
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    pfr += 1
                    pfs_c[j] += 1
                    tot_apples += 1
                pfs_r[j] = pfr
            self.pfsum_row.append(pfs_r)
            self.pfsum_col.append(pfs_c)
        
        if tot_apples < k:
            return 0
        
        if k = 1:
            return 1
        
        return self.getWays(0, 0, k) % (1000000007)
    
    
    @cache
    def getWays(self, i, j, k):
        if k == 1:
            found = False
            for c in range(j, this.c):
                apple_in_region = this.pfsum_col[this.r - 1][c]
                if i > 0:
                     apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_col[i - 1][c]
                if apple_in_region:
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                return 1
            return 0
        else:
            cannot_cut = True
            nr = i
            t_cnt = 0
            while nr < this.r - 1:
                while nr < this.r - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = this.pfsum_row[nr][this.c - 1]
                    if j > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_row[nr][j - 1]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nr += 1
                        
                if nr < this.r - 1:
                    t_cnt += this.getWays(nr + 1, j, k - 1)
                nr += 1
                
            cannot_cut = True
            nc = j
            while nc < this.c - 1:
                while nc < this.c - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = this.pfsum_col[this.r - 1][nc]
                    if i > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_col[i - 1][nc]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nc += 1
                
                if nc < this.c - 1:
                    t_cnt += this.getWays(i, nc + 1, k - 1)
                nc += 1
            
            return t_cnt
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        R, C = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        pref = [[0]*(C+1) for _ in range(R+1)]
        for i in range(R):
            row_sum = 0
            for j in range(C):
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    row_sum += 1
                pref[i+1][j+1] = pref[i][j+1] + row_sum
        # Actually pref[i+1][j+1] = pref[i][j+1] + row_sum where row_sum is apples in row i up to j. This equals total apples in rows 0..i, cols 0..j. Let's verify: pref[i+1][j+1] = pref[i][j+1] + row_sum. pref[i][j+1] is rows 0..i-1, cols 0..j. row_sum is row i cols 0..j. Yes.
        MOD = 1000000007

        def apples(r1,c1,r2,c2):
            if r1>r2 or c1>c2: return 0
            return pref[r2+1][c2+1] - pref[r1][c2+1] - pref[r2+1][c1] + pref[r1][c1]

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i,j,k):
            if k == 1:
                return 1 if apples(i,j,R-1,C-1) > 0 else 0
            ans = 0
            # horizontal cuts
            for x in range(i, R-1):
                if apples(i,j,x,C-1) > 0:
                    ans += dp(x+1,j,k-1)
            # vertical cuts
            for y in range(j, C-1):
                if apples(i,j,R-1,y) > 0:
                    ans += dp(i,y+1,k-1)
            return ans % MOD

        if sum(row.count('A') for row in pizza) < k:
            return 0
        return dp(0,0,k) % MOD
```

```python
class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        tot_apples = 0
        self.pfsum_row = []
        self.pfsum_col = []
        
        for i in range(self.r):
            pfr = 0
            pfs_r = [0] * self.c
            pfs_c = [0] * self.c
            for j in range(self.c):
                if i > 0:
                    pfs_c[j] += self.pfsum_col[i - 1][j]
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    pfr += 1
                    pfs_c[j] += 1
                    tot_apples += 1
                pfs_r[j] = pfr
            self.pfsum_row.append(pfs_r)
            self.pfsum_col.append(pfs_c)
        
        if tot_apples < k:
            return 0
        
        if k = 1:
            return 1
        
        return self.getWays(0, 0, k) % (1000000007)
    
    
    @cache
    def getWays(self, i, j, k):
        if k == 1:
            found = False
            for c in range(j, this.c):
                apple_in_region = this.pfsum_col[this.r - 1][c]
                if i > 0:
                     apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_col[i - 1][c]
                if apple_in_region:
                    found = True
                    break

                return 1
            return 0
```

Let's write final code:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        R, C = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        MOD = 10**9 + 7

        pref = [[0] * (C + 1) for _ in range(R + 1)]
        for i in range(R):
            row_apples = 0
            for j in range(C):
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    row_apples += 1
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = pref[i][j + 1] + row_apples

        def apples(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> int:
            return pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1] - pref[r1][c2 + 1] - pref[r2 + 1][c1] + pref[r1][c1]

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if apples(i, j, R - 1, C - 1) > 0 else 0

            ans = 0
            for r in range(i, R - 1):
                if apples(i, j, r, C - 1) > 0:
                    ans += dp(r + 1, j, pieces - 1)
            for c in range(j, C - 1):
                if apples(i, j, R - 1, c) > 0:
                    ans += dp(i, c + 1, pieces - 1)
            return ans % MOD

        if pref[R][C] < k:
            return 0
        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

Need verify `pref[R][C]` equals total apples. With our construction, yes. Let's test with pizza ["A..","AAA","..."]: pref[3][3] = 4. Good.

Potential issue: `pref[i + 1][j + 1] = pref[i][j + 1] + row_apples` uses `pref[i][j+1]` from previous row, but `pref[i][j+1]` is already computed because outer loop i increasing, inner j increasing. For i=0, pref[0][...] zero. For i>0, pref[i][j+1] computed in previous row. Good.

Let's think if `row_apples` should be reset each row. Yes.

Let's compare with standard prefix formula: `pref[i+1][j+1] = pref[i][j+1] + pref[i+1][j] - pref[i][j] + (pizza[i][j]=='A')`. Our formula `pref[i][j+1] + row_apples` where row_apples is cumulative apples in current row up to j. Is it always equal? Let's prove by induction:
pref[i+1][j+1] = sum_{a=0..i} sum_{b=0..j} apple(a,b)
= sum_{a=0..i-1} sum_{b=0..j} apple(a,b) + sum_{b=0..j} apple(i,b)
= pref[i][j+1] + row_apples. Yes. So correct. It doesn't need `pref[i+1][j]` because row_apples accumulates horizontally. Good.

Potential issue: `apples` function with r1,c1,r2,c2 inclusive. For vertical cut, left piece rows i..R-1, cols j..c. We call `apples(i,j,R-1,c)`. Good. For horizontal, top piece rows i..r, cols j..C-1. Good.

Horizontal r=0: top row0 col2 0 skip. r=1: top rows0-1 col2 has apple (1,2)=1 -> dp(2,2,1). Region row2 col2 0 ->0. Vertical none (C-1=2, c from j=2 to 1 none). So dp(0,2,2)=0. Contribution 0.

Let's decide final code structure:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        R, C = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        MOD = 1000000007

        pref = [[0] * (C + 1) for _ in range(R + 1)]
        for i in range(R):
            for j in range(C):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        if pref[R][C] < k:
            return 0

        def apples(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> int:
            return (
                pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1]
                - pref[r1][c2 + 1]
                - pref[r2 + 1][c1]
                + pref[r1][c1]
            )

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if apples(i, j, R - 1, C - 1) > 0 else 0

            ans = 0
            for r in range(i, R - 1):
                if apples(i, j, r, C - 1) > 0:
                    ans += dp(r + 1, j, pieces - 1)
            for c in range(j, C - 1):
                if apples(i, j, R - 1, c) > 0:
                    ans += dp(i, c + 1, pieces - 1)
            return ans % MOD

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

```
    while nr < this.r - 1 and cannot_cut:
        ...
        if apple_in_region:
            cannot_cut = False
        else:
            nr += 1
    if nr < this.r - 1:
        t_cnt += this.getWays(nr + 1, j, k - 1)
    nr += 1
```
They set cannot_cut = True once before the outer while, not inside. So after first valid cut found, cannot_cut becomes False and remains False for all subsequent nr. That means for all later nr, they add dp without checking if top piece has apple. That is a serious bug. Our simple loop checks each r individually. Good.

Also, the inner while logic is weird: If top piece at current nr has no apple, increment nr. If top piece has apple, set cannot_cut=False but do not increment nr. Then after inner while, if nr < r-1, add dp for that nr. Then nr += 1. So it checks each row? Actually after first valid, cannot_cut False, inner while skipped, so subsequent rows not checked. So bug. Our loop fixes.

Similarly for vertical.

Let's write final code now in my mind:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        R, C = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        MOD = 1000000007

        pref = [[0] * (C + 1) for _ in range(R + 1)]
        for i in range(R):
            for j in range(C):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        if pref[R][C] < k:
            return 0
        if k == 1:
            return 1

        def apples(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> int:
            return (
                pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1]
                - pref[r1][c2 + 1]
                - pref[r2 + 1][c1]
                + pref[r1][c1]
            )

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if apples(i, j, R - 1, C - 1) > 0 else 0

            ans = 0
            for r in range(i, R - 1):
                if apples(i, j, r, C - 1) > 0:
                    ans = (ans + dp(r + 1, j, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            for c in range(j, C - 1):
                if apples(i, j, R - 1, c) > 0:
                    ans = (ans + dp(i, c + 1, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            return ans

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

This is correct.
